This generalizes the symmetric projection <math alttext="M=QSQ^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S5.p6.m5" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M=QSQ^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> from Section <a class="ltx_ref" href="#S2" title="2 Projection of Corpus-Level Statistics into Sequence Context ‣ Self-Attention as Distributional Projection: A Unified Interpretation of Transformer Architecture"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>: the learned factorization <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S5.p6.m6" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> plays the same structural role as the corpus matrix <math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S5.p6.m7" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math>, but now admits asymmetric relationships.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S5.p7">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S5.p7.1">This recovers the familiar query–key compatibility matrix from Transformers: <math alttext="Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S5.p7.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>

## 6 Learned Attention and Multi-Head Decomposition

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.p1.1">We can now fully articulate the connection to the standard transformer self-attention mechanism. The complete computation:</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex30">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="\mathrm{softmax}\left(\frac{Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V_{\text{att}}," class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex30.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>softmax</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{softmax}\left(\frac{Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V_{\text{att}},</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.p1.2">where <math alttext="Q_{\text{att}}=QW_{Q}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{\text{att}}=QW_{Q}</annotation></semantics></math>, <math alttext="K_{\text{att}}=QW_{K}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K_{\text{att}}=QW_{K}</annotation></semantics></math>, and <math alttext="V_{\text{att}}=QW_{V}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V_{\text{att}}=QW_{V}</annotation></semantics></math>, emerges as the natural extension of our distributional semantics foundation. Here, <math alttext="d_{k}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p1.m4" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> represents the <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S6.p1.2.1">dimension of the key and query vectors</span>, which controls the capacity of the attention mechanism and provides numerical stability through the scaling factor <math alttext="1/\sqrt{d_{k}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p1.m5" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/\sqrt{d_{k}}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.p2.1">The progression from our initial symmetric projection to the full transformer architecture is now complete:</p>
<ul class="ltx_itemize" id="S6.I1">
<li class="ltx_item" id="S6.I1.i1" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.I1.i1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.I1.i1.p1.1">The core contextual weighting mechanism derives from <math alttext="M=QSQ^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.I1.i1.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M=QSQ^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math></p>
</div></li>
<li class="ltx_item" id="S6.I1.i2" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.I1.i2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.I1.i2.p1.1">Positional sensitivity comes from the augmented selector <math alttext="Q^{\prime}=Q+P" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>P</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q^{\prime}=Q+P</annotation></semantics></math></p>
</div></li>
<li class="ltx_item" id="S6.I1.i3" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.I1.i3.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.I1.i3.p1.1">Directional relationships emerge from the asymmetric factorization <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.I1.i3.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math></p>
</div></li>
<li class="ltx_item" id="S6.I1.i4" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span>
<div class="ltx_para" id="S6.I1.i4.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.I1.i4.p1.1">The value projection <math alttext="W_{V}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.I1.i4.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{V}</annotation></semantics></math> generalizes the vocabulary associations in <math alttext="QS" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.I1.i4.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">QS</annotation></semantics></math></p>
</div></li>
</ul>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.p3">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.p3.1">Thus, the transformer self-attention block <math alttext="\mathrm{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.p3.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>softmax</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathrm{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})</annotation></semantics></math> represents the learned, refined version of the fundamental projection principle we analyzed from distributional semantics.</p>
</div><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S6.SS1">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">6.1 </span>From Projection to Learned Self-Attention</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p1.1">Having established the theoretical foundation, we now show how the full Transformer self-attention mechanism emerges as the natural learned extension of our distributional projection framework.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p2.1">Let <math alttext="H\in\mathbb{R}^{R\times d_{\text{model}}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p2.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H\in\mathbb{R}^{R\times d_{\text{model}}}</annotation></semantics></math> be the current layer’s token representations. The model learns three projections that generalize our earlier components:</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p3">
<table class="ltx_equationgroup ltx_eqn_eqnarray ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.EGx2">
<tbody id="S6.Ex31"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle Q_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle Q_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{Q},\quad W_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{Q},\quad W_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
<tbody id="S6.Ex32"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle K_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle K_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{K},\quad W_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{K},\quad W_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
<tbody id="S6.Ex33"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle V_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle V_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{V},\quad W_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{v}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{V},\quad W_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{v}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p4">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p4.1">where <math alttext="d_{k}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p4.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is the dimension of key and query vectors, and <math alttext="d_{v}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p4.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{v}</annotation></semantics></math> is the dimension of value vectors. The asymmetric compatibility scores</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p5">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex34">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="S_{\text{att}}=Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}=HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex34.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{\text{att}}=Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}=HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p6">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p6.1">generalize our earlier projection <math alttext="QSQ^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p6.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">QSQ^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>, but now with learned directional relationships through the asymmetric factorization <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p6.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p7">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p7.1">Applying row-wise softmax with scaling for numerical stability:</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p8">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex35">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="A=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{S_{\text{att}}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex35.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mtext>softmax</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>S</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{S_{\text{att}}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p9">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p9.1">produces attention weights where each row represents how much each token should attend to others in the sequence. The final contextualized representation:</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p10">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex36">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y=AV_{\text{att}}=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex36.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mtext>softmax</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y=AV_{\text{att}}=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p11">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p11.1">represents the complete Transformer self-attention layer—the learned, directional refinement of our fundamental projection principle.</p>
</div>
</section><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S6.SS2">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">6.2 </span>Multi-head attention</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.1">A single compatibility map <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> cannot capture all types of contextual relation in language. Transformers therefore use <math alttext="h" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math> heads in parallel. For head <math alttext="i" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>,</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex37">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Q^{(i)}=HW_{Q}^{(i)},\quad K^{(i)}=HW_{K}^{(i)},\quad V^{(i)}=HW_{V}^{(i)}," class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex37.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>K</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q^{(i)}=HW_{Q}^{(i)},\quad K^{(i)}=HW_{K}^{(i)},\quad V^{(i)}=HW_{V}^{(i)},</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.2">and</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex38">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y^{(i)}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{Q^{(i)}{K^{(i)}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V^{(i)}." class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex38.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mpadded style="width:3.720em;" width="3.720em"><mi>softmax</mi></mpadded><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>K</mi><mrow></mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow></mrow><mo>⊤</mo></mmultiscripts></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y^{(i)}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{Q^{(i)}{K^{(i)}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V^{(i)}.</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.3">These head-specific outputs are concatenated and linearly recombined:</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex39">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y=[Y^{(1)}\|Y^{(2)}\|\cdots\|Y^{(h)}]W_{O}" class="ltx_math_unparsed" display="block" id="S6.Ex39.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∥</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.0835em">∥</mo><mo lspace="0.0835em" rspace="0em">⋯</mo><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∥</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>O</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y=[Y^{(1)}\|Y^{(2)}\|\cdots\|Y^{(h)}]W_{O}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.4">with <math alttext="W_{O}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m4" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{O}</annotation></semantics></math> a learned projection.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS2.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p2.1">From the projection viewpoint, multi-head attention corresponds to factorizing <math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math> into multiple low-rank relational components <math alttext="S^{(1)},\dots,S^{(h)}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>S</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>S</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S^{(1)},\dots,S^{(h)}</annotation></semantics></math>, each one specializing in a different pattern: syntactic dependency, coreference, topical cohesion, discourse linkage, etc. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib8" title="">8</a>]</cite>. The model does not commit to a single <math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math>; it learns several structured approximations in parallel, and then recombines them.</p>
</div>
</section><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S6.SS3">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">6.3 </span>Cross-attention as cross-projection</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS3.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p1.1">In encoder–decoder models, cross-attention is the same algebra, except that queries come from one sequence (e.g., target side) and keys/values from another (e.g., source side). If <math alttext="H_{\text{src}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H_{\text{src}}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math alttext="H_{\text{tgt}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H_{\text{tgt}}</annotation></semantics></math> are the source and target representations, then:</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex40">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y_{\text{cross}}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{H_{\text{tgt}}W_{Q}(H_{\text{src}}W_{K})^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(H_{\text{src}}W_{V})." class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex40.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Y</mi><mtext>cross</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mpadded style="width:3.720em;" width="3.720em"><mi>softmax</mi></mpadded><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y_{\text{cross}}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{H_{\text{tgt}}W_{Q}(H_{\text{src}}W_{K})^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(H_{\text{src}}W_{V}).</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p1.2">This is just <math alttext="Q_{\text{tgt}}K_{\text{src}}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>src</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{\text{tgt}}K_{\text{src}}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> followed by mixing <math alttext="V_{\text{src}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m4" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V_{\text{src}}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS3.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p2.1">Within this projection framework, cross-attention performs an asymmetric mapping from the relational structure of the source sequence to the subspace defined by the target sequence.</p>
</div>
</section>

## 6.1 From Projection to Learned Self-Attention

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p1.1">Having established the theoretical foundation, we now show how the full Transformer self-attention mechanism emerges as the natural learned extension of our distributional projection framework.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p2.1">Let <math alttext="H\in\mathbb{R}^{R\times d_{\text{model}}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p2.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H\in\mathbb{R}^{R\times d_{\text{model}}}</annotation></semantics></math> be the current layer’s token representations. The model learns three projections that generalize our earlier components:</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p3">
<table class="ltx_equationgroup ltx_eqn_eqnarray ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.EGx2">
<tbody id="S6.Ex31"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle Q_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle Q_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{Q},\quad W_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex31.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{Q},\quad W_{Q}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
<tbody id="S6.Ex32"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle K_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle K_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{K},\quad W_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex32.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{K},\quad W_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{k}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
<tbody id="S6.Ex33"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle V_{\text{att}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle V_{\text{att}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_center ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle=" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mo>=</mo><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle=</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math alttext="\displaystyle HW_{V},\quad W_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{v}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.Ex33.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msup><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><msub><mi>d</mi><mtext>model</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></msub></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle HW_{V},\quad W_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\text{model}}\times d_{v}}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p4">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p4.1">where <math alttext="d_{k}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p4.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{k}</annotation></semantics></math> is the dimension of key and query vectors, and <math alttext="d_{v}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p4.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>v</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">d_{v}</annotation></semantics></math> is the dimension of value vectors. The asymmetric compatibility scores</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p5">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex34">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="S_{\text{att}}=Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}=HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex34.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>att</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_{\text{att}}=Q_{\text{att}}K_{\text{att}}^{\top}=HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p6">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p6.1">generalize our earlier projection <math alttext="QSQ^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p6.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">QSQ^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>, but now with learned directional relationships through the asymmetric factorization <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS1.p6.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p7">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p7.1">Applying row-wise softmax with scaling for numerical stability:</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p8">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex35">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="A=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{S_{\text{att}}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex35.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mtext>softmax</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>S</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{S_{\text{att}}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p9">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p9.1">produces attention weights where each row represents how much each token should attend to others in the sequence. The final contextualized representation:</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p10">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex36">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y=AV_{\text{att}}=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})" class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex36.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>att</mtext></msub></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mtext>softmax</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y=AV_{\text{att}}=\text{softmax}\left(\frac{HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(HW_{V})</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS1.p11">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS1.p11.1">represents the complete Transformer self-attention layer—the learned, directional refinement of our fundamental projection principle.</p>
</div>

## 6.2 Multi-head attention

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.1">A single compatibility map <math alttext="W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> cannot capture all types of contextual relation in language. Transformers therefore use <math alttext="h" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math> heads in parallel. For head <math alttext="i" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>,</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex37">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Q^{(i)}=HW_{Q}^{(i)},\quad K^{(i)}=HW_{K}^{(i)},\quad V^{(i)}=HW_{V}^{(i)}," class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex37.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>K</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow><mo rspace="1.167em">,</mo><mrow><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q^{(i)}=HW_{Q}^{(i)},\quad K^{(i)}=HW_{K}^{(i)},\quad V^{(i)}=HW_{V}^{(i)},</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.2">and</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex38">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y^{(i)}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{Q^{(i)}{K^{(i)}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V^{(i)}." class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex38.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mpadded style="width:3.720em;" width="3.720em"><mi>softmax</mi></mpadded><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>K</mi><mrow></mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mrow></mrow><mo>⊤</mo></mmultiscripts></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>V</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y^{(i)}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{Q^{(i)}{K^{(i)}}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)V^{(i)}.</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.3">These head-specific outputs are concatenated and linearly recombined:</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex39">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y=[Y^{(1)}\|Y^{(2)}\|\cdots\|Y^{(h)}]W_{O}" class="ltx_math_unparsed" display="block" id="S6.Ex39.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∥</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.0835em">∥</mo><mo lspace="0.0835em" rspace="0em">⋯</mo><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0.167em">∥</mo><msup><mi>Y</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>O</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y=[Y^{(1)}\|Y^{(2)}\|\cdots\|Y^{(h)}]W_{O}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p1.4">with <math alttext="W_{O}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p1.m4" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>O</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{O}</annotation></semantics></math> a learned projection.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS2.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS2.p2.1">From the projection viewpoint, multi-head attention corresponds to factorizing <math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math> into multiple low-rank relational components <math alttext="S^{(1)},\dots,S^{(h)}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>S</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>S</mi><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>h</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S^{(1)},\dots,S^{(h)}</annotation></semantics></math>, each one specializing in a different pattern: syntactic dependency, coreference, topical cohesion, discourse linkage, etc. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib8" title="">8</a>]</cite>. The model does not commit to a single <math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS2.p2.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math>; it learns several structured approximations in parallel, and then recombines them.</p>
</div>

## 6.3 Cross-attention as cross-projection

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS3.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p1.1">In encoder–decoder models, cross-attention is the same algebra, except that queries come from one sequence (e.g., target side) and keys/values from another (e.g., source side). If <math alttext="H_{\text{src}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H_{\text{src}}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math alttext="H_{\text{tgt}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H_{\text{tgt}}</annotation></semantics></math> are the source and target representations, then:</p>
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S6.Ex40">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="Y_{\text{cross}}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{H_{\text{tgt}}W_{Q}(H_{\text{src}}W_{K})^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(H_{\text{src}}W_{V})." class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S6.Ex40.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>Y</mi><mtext>cross</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mpadded style="width:3.720em;" width="3.720em"><mi>softmax</mi></mpadded><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo>(</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mfrac><mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Y_{\text{cross}}=\mathrm{softmax}\!\left(\frac{H_{\text{tgt}}W_{Q}(H_{\text{src}}W_{K})^{\top}}{\sqrt{d_{k}}}\right)(H_{\text{src}}W_{V}).</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p1.2">This is just <math alttext="Q_{\text{tgt}}K_{\text{src}}^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><mtext>tgt</mtext></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>K</mi><mtext>src</mtext><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Q_{\text{tgt}}K_{\text{src}}^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> followed by mixing <math alttext="V_{\text{src}}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S6.SS3.p1.m4" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>V</mi><mtext>src</mtext></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V_{\text{src}}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S6.SS3.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S6.SS3.p2.1">Within this projection framework, cross-attention performs an asymmetric mapping from the relational structure of the source sequence to the subspace defined by the target sequence.</p>
</div>

## 7 Related Work

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p1.1">The framework presented here connects two long-standing lines of research:</p>
<ol class="ltx_enumerate" id="S7.I1">
<li class="ltx_item" id="S7.I1.i1" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.I1.i1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.I1.i1.p1.1">The study of attention mechanisms in neural sequence models</p>
</div></li>
<li class="ltx_item" id="S7.I1.i2" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span>
<div class="ltx_para" id="S7.I1.i2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.I1.i2.p1.1">The linear-algebraic tradition of distributional semantics and matrix factorization in natural language processing.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p1.2">The goal is to situate the present analysis within both traditions and within recent theoretical analyses of attention and representation learning.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p2.1">Self-attention was first introduced as a differentiable alignment mechanism for neural machine translation <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib2" title="">2</a>]</cite>, where it allowed a decoder to focus selectively on encoder states relevant to each generated word.
It was then generalized into the transformer architecture <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib9" title="">9</a>]</cite>, in which attention became the core representational primitive rather than an auxiliary alignment tool.
Subsequent work examined attention’s internal structure, identifying head specialization, syntactic alignment, and interpretability patterns that arise during training <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib3" title="">3</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib8" title="">8</a>]</cite>.
These studies established attention as both a computational and a linguistic phenomenon, but typically treated its algebraic form as given.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p3">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p3.1">A parallel and older thread of research—distributional semantics—treated word meaning as emerging from the co-occurrence structure of language data.
Methods such as latent semantic analysis, positive pointwise mutual information (PMI), and global vector embeddings <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib7" title="">7</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib6" title="">6</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib1" title="">1</a>]</cite> modeled this structure explicitly through matrix factorization, capturing semantic similarity and analogy relations in linear spaces.
These approaches anticipated many of the representational properties later rediscovered in deep embeddings, but lacked a mechanism for adapting corpus-level statistics to specific sentence contexts.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p4">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p4.1">Recent theoretical work has interpreted attention through various mathematical frameworks, including structured linear operators and kernel methods <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib5" title="">5</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib4" title="">4</a>]</cite>.
In particular, Zhang et al. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib10" title="">10</a>]</cite> analyze the learning dynamics of linear attention, providing another mathematical perspective on how transformers capture contextual relationships. Our contribution complements these analyses by showing how the self-attention equations directly from corpus projection algebra: attention emerges as the unique asymmetric refinement of a co-occurrence operator restricted to an input sequence.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p5">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p5.1">While the connection between distributional semantics and neural embeddings is well-established <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib7" title="">7</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib6" title="">6</a>]</cite>, and attention mechanisms have been analyzed through various theoretical lenses <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib5" title="">5</a>, <a class="ltx_ref" href="#bib.bib4" title="">4</a>]</cite>, our work provides a systematic analysis of the self-attention mechanism from corpus projection principles.
Unlike prior work that analyzes attention through kernel methods or learning dynamics, we derive the attention mechanism from first principles of distributional semantics.
The mathematical pathway from the symmetric projection <math alttext="QSQ^{\top}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S7.p5.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">QSQ^{\top}</annotation></semantics></math> through asymmetric generalization to the full Transformer attention block offers a novel perspective that unifies these research traditions.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S7.p6">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S7.p6.1">In this sense, the present work links the empirical lineage of transformer modeling with the theoretical lineage of distributional semantics.
Both begin with linear structure on word co-occurrence; both obtain contextual meaning by projecting or factoring that structure into lower-dimensional subspaces.
The difference is that transformers make those projections directional, learnable, and order-sensitive, thereby recovering classical distributional semantics as a limiting case within a broader, trainable framework.</p>
</div>

## 8 Discussion and Implications

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.p1.1">This paper has presented a unified mathematical interpretation of self-attention through the lens of distributional projection. The Transformer architecture can be understood as a sequence of structured refinements to a core distributional principle:</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.p2">
<table class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table" id="S8.Ex41">
<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math alttext="S\to QSQ^{\top}\to QW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}Q^{\top}\to(Q+P)S(Q+P)^{\top}\to\text{softmax}\Big(HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}/\sqrt{d_{k}}\Big)(HW_{V})." class="ltx_Math" display="block" id="S8.Ex41.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>Q</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>P</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>P</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><mrow><mtext>softmax</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em" minsize="1.600em">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msubsup><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>H</mi><mo>⊤</mo></msup></mrow><mo>/</mo><msqrt><msub><mi>d</mi><mi>k</mi></msub></msqrt></mrow><mo maxsize="1.600em" minsize="1.600em">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>V</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S\to QSQ^{\top}\to QW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}Q^{\top}\to(Q+P)S(Q+P)^{\top}\to\text{softmax}\Big(HW_{Q}W_{K}^{\top}H^{\top}/\sqrt{d_{k}}\Big)(HW_{V}).</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.p3">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.p3.1">Each step in this chain refines the same core operation–projecting global distributional structure into a sequence-specific, directional, and order-aware subspace.</p>
</div><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S8.SS1">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">8.1 </span>Reproducibility</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS1.p1.1">This work presents a theoretical analysis of the self-attention mechanism
through the lens of distributional semantics. All mathematical derivations
follow from standard linear algebra operations on the co-occurrence
matrices defined in the text. The illustrative examples
(Sections <a class="ltx_ref" href="#S2.SS5" title="2.5 Illustrative Examples ‣ 2 Projection of Corpus-Level Statistics into Sequence Context ‣ Self-Attention as Distributional Projection: A Unified Interpretation of Transformer Architecture"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2.5</span></a> and <a class="ltx_ref" href="#S3.SS2" title="3.2 Illustrating the Projection 𝐸=𝑀(𝑄⁢𝑆) ‣ 3 Extending Local Context to Vocabulary-Level Prediction ‣ Self-Attention as Distributional Projection: A Unified Interpretation of Transformer Architecture"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>) use a small, explicitly defined corpus shown in the paper to
demonstrate the conceptual mechanism, and all calculations can be verified
by hand or with basic matrix operations. No experimental code, trained
models, or external datasets are required to reproduce this analysis.</p>
</div>
</section><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S8.SS2">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">8.2 </span>Limitations</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS2.p1.1">This framework provides a conceptual interpretation of self-attention
rather than a prescriptive model for how transformers should be designed
or trained. The connection between corpus-level co-occurrence statistics
(<math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math>) and learned projection matrices (<math alttext="W_{Q}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}</annotation></semantics></math>, <math alttext="W_{K}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{K}</annotation></semantics></math>) is conceptual: we show
they have the same algebraic form, not that one should be initialized
from the other. The examples illustrate the mathematical mechanism using
a simplified corpus to clarify the operations, and do not constitute
empirical validation on realistic data.</p>
</div>
</section><section class="ltx_subsection" id="S8.SS3">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">8.3 </span>Implications for Understanding Transformers</h3>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p1.1">This projection framework provides mathematical insight into why self-attention works effectively for language modeling:</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p2.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p2.1.1">Architectural Intuition:</span> The query-key-value structure, often presented through information-retrieval metaphors, can be understood as a natural consequence of projecting corpus-level associations into sequence-specific contexts with directional asymmetry.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p3">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p3.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p3.1.1">Unified Interpretation:</span> Components like positional encodings and multi-head attention, typically introduced as separate mechanisms, emerge naturally as refinements of the same projection principle.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p4">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p4.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p4.1.1">Connection to Distributional Semantics:</span> The framework bridges classical distributional approaches (GloVe, word2vec) and modern neural architectures, showing how Transformers extend rather than replace earlier semantic models.</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p5">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p5.1">This interpretation does not replace empirical analysis or suggest modifications to existing architectures. Rather, it provides a conceptual lens for understanding why the Transformer’s particular design has proven effective.</p>
</div>
</section>

## 8.1 Reproducibility

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS1.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS1.p1.1">This work presents a theoretical analysis of the self-attention mechanism
through the lens of distributional semantics. All mathematical derivations
follow from standard linear algebra operations on the co-occurrence
matrices defined in the text. The illustrative examples
(Sections <a class="ltx_ref" href="#S2.SS5" title="2.5 Illustrative Examples ‣ 2 Projection of Corpus-Level Statistics into Sequence Context ‣ Self-Attention as Distributional Projection: A Unified Interpretation of Transformer Architecture"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2.5</span></a> and <a class="ltx_ref" href="#S3.SS2" title="3.2 Illustrating the Projection 𝐸=𝑀(𝑄⁢𝑆) ‣ 3 Extending Local Context to Vocabulary-Level Prediction ‣ Self-Attention as Distributional Projection: A Unified Interpretation of Transformer Architecture"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>) use a small, explicitly defined corpus shown in the paper to
demonstrate the conceptual mechanism, and all calculations can be verified
by hand or with basic matrix operations. No experimental code, trained
models, or external datasets are required to reproduce this analysis.</p>
</div>

## 8.2 Limitations

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS2.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS2.p1.1">This framework provides a conceptual interpretation of self-attention
rather than a prescriptive model for how transformers should be designed
or trained. The connection between corpus-level co-occurrence statistics
(<math alttext="S" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m1" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>S</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S</annotation></semantics></math>) and learned projection matrices (<math alttext="W_{Q}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m2" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{Q}</annotation></semantics></math>, <math alttext="W_{K}" class="ltx_Math" display="inline" id="S8.SS2.p1.m3" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>K</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{K}</annotation></semantics></math>) is conceptual: we show
they have the same algebraic form, not that one should be initialized
from the other. The examples illustrate the mathematical mechanism using
a simplified corpus to clarify the operations, and do not constitute
empirical validation on realistic data.</p>
</div>

## 8.3 Implications for Understanding Transformers

<div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p1">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p1.1">This projection framework provides mathematical insight into why self-attention works effectively for language modeling:</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p2">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p2.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p2.1.1">Architectural Intuition:</span> The query-key-value structure, often presented through information-retrieval metaphors, can be understood as a natural consequence of projecting corpus-level associations into sequence-specific contexts with directional asymmetry.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p3">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p3.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p3.1.1">Unified Interpretation:</span> Components like positional encodings and multi-head attention, typically introduced as separate mechanisms, emerge naturally as refinements of the same projection principle.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p4">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p4.1"><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold" id="S8.SS3.p4.1.1">Connection to Distributional Semantics:</span> The framework bridges classical distributional approaches (GloVe, word2vec) and modern neural architectures, showing how Transformers extend rather than replace earlier semantic models.</p>
</div><div class="ltx_para ltx_noindent" id="S8.SS3.p5">
<p class="ltx_p" id="S8.SS3.p5.1">This interpretation does not replace empirical analysis or suggest modifications to existing architectures. Rather, it provides a conceptual lens for understanding why the Transformer’s particular design has proven effective.</p>
</div>
